  content: |
      # PHASE 12 — PRODUCTION OBSERVABILITY, ALERTS & OPERATIONAL HARDENING COMPLETE (2026-10-12)
      
      - **Developer tooling — Agentation (2026-10-12):** ADR-025 accepted by explicit
        user authorization. Agentation 3.1.2 is installed as an `apps/web`
        development dependency, loaded through a client-only development overlay,
        and aliased out of production webpack builds. No MCP server or annotation
        endpoint is configured.
      
      - **Objective**: Implement authoritative Phase 12 — Production Observability, Alerts & Operational Hardening per `technical_design/28_OBSERVABILITY_ARCHITECTURE.md`, `INITIAL_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` §19/§27/§28, `ADR-001`–`ADR-021`, and `NFR-01`–`NFR-15`.
      - **Architectural Deliverables**:
        1. **OpenTelemetry-Compliant Tracing & W3C TraceContext (`packages/observability`)**:
           - `src/tracing/context.ts`: Complete W3C Level 1 TraceContext parsing, formatting, and header extraction/injection (`traceparent`, `tracestate`, `x-trace-id`).
           - `src/tracing/tracer.ts`: In-memory OpenTelemetry tracer provider, `SpanImpl`, `InMemorySpanExporter`, and `withSpan()` execution lifecycle helper with automatic exception and duration tracking.
           - Cardinality guard: Statement shape/hash only for DB spans, route template only for HTTP spans, no bound parameter values or customer secrets.
        2. **Structured JSON Logging with PII Scrubbing (`packages/observability`)**:
           - `src/logging/logger.ts`: High-performance structured logger with `trace_id`, `span_id`, `request_id`, `actor_type`, and `event` (`domain.action`).
           - Proactive multi-layer PII sanitization: Recursively scrubs emails, phone numbers, credit card sequences, authentication tokens, passwords, and raw biometric/camera frames.
        3. **Prometheus-Compatible Metrics & Strict Cardinality Protection (`packages/observability`)**:
           - `src/metrics/registry.ts`: Type-safe `Counter`, `Gauge`, and `Histogram` instruments with percentile computation (p95).
           - Automated cardinality check: Throws `CardinalityViolationError` if high-cardinality keys (`user_id`, `email`, `sku`, `url`, `token`, etc.) are attached to metric labels.
        4. **Sentry Error Reporting & Client Boundary (`packages/observability`)**:
           - `src/error/sentry.ts`: Sentry client with data minimization, sanitization, bounded breadcrumbs ring buffer, and non-blocking failure semantics.
           - `src/error/client.ts`: Isomorphic/Browser-safe error capturing facade (`captureClientError`), ensuring zero Node-specific runtime code enters client bundles (preserving PG-5 bundle budgets).
           - Route error boundaries: Root error boundary (`apps/web/src/app/error.tsx`) and catalog route boundaries (`CatalogErrorView.tsx`) wired with Sentry capture and luxury customer recovery fallbacks.
        5. **Production SLO Definitions & Alert Evaluator (`packages/observability`)**:
           - `src/slo/evaluator.ts`: Authoritative production SLOs:
             - `SLO-AVAIL-01`: Storefront & API Availability $\ge 99.9\%$ (Alert `ALERT-P1-AVAILABILITY-DROP`)
             - `SLO-LAT-01`: Catalog Query Latency p95 $\le 400\text{ms}$ (Alert `ALERT-P2-CATALOG-LATENCY-BREACH`)
             - `SLO-AI-GROUNDING`: AI Concierge Grounded Ratio $\ge 0.60$ (Alert `ALERT-P1-AI-GROUNDING-COLLAPSE`)
             - `SLO-WHATSAPP-DELIVERY`: WhatsApp Webhook Success Rate $\ge 99.0\%$ (Alert `ALERT-P1-WHATSAPP-WEBHOOK-FAILURES`)
             - `SLO-DB-CONN`: Database Pool Wait p95 $\le 50\text{ms}$ (Alert `ALERT-P1-DB-CONNECTIVITY-TIMEOUT`)
           - `AlertEvaluator`: Sliding-window evaluation transitioning alerts from `FIRING` to `RESOLVED`.
        6. **Health Probes & Metrics Exposition (`apps/web`)**:
           - `GET /api/v1/health/live`: Process liveness probe returning 200 and uptime.
           - `GET /api/v1/health/ready`: Dependency readiness probe inspecting database pool connectivity and memory health.
           - `GET /api/v1/health/metrics`: Prometheus/OpenMetrics formatted endpoint for low-cardinality metric scraping.
        7. **Operational Runbooks (`docs/runbooks/`)**:
           - `RB-01-ELEVATED-ERROR-RATE.md`: Triage, Sentry trace investigation, and mitigation for availability drops.
           - `RB-02-HIGH-LATENCY-REGRESSION.md`: Diagnostics for slow query and facet regression.
           - `RB-03-DATABASE-CONNECTIVITY-TIMEOUT.md`: Neon compute resumption, connection pooling, and lock triage.
           - `RB-04-AI-PROVIDER-OUTAGE-FAILOVER.md`: Handling grounding collapse and model gateway provider fallback.
           - `RB-05-WHATSAPP-WEBHOOK-FAILURES.md`: Signature verification, raw body integrity, and idempotency recovery.
           - `RB-06-SLO-ERROR-BUDGET-BURN.md`: Error budget policy, burn rate thresholds, and freeze protocols.
      - **Verification**:
        - Workspace typecheck (`pnpm -r run typecheck`): 0 errors across all 10 packages.
        - Test suite (`pnpm test`): 438 passing tests across monorepo (12 observability tests, 396 web tests, 30 db/contracts tests).
        - Next.js production build (`pnpm --filter @maihan/web run build`): 100% successful.
        - Storefront bundle budgets (`pnpm --filter @maihan/web run check:budgets`): PASSED (Category 138.0 KB $\le$ 170 KB, Product 141.6 KB $\le$ 190 KB).
      
      # PHASE 11 — WEBAR TRY-ON & 3D VIEWER COMPLETE (2026-10-12)
      
      - **Objective**: Implement authoritative Phase 11 — WebAR Try-On & 3D Viewer per `INITIAL_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` §18, `technical_design/21_WEBAR_ARCHITECTURE.md`, `ADR-004`, `ADR-011`, and `NFR-06`.
      - **Architectural Deliverables**:
        1. **WebAR Contracts & Technical Budgets (`packages/contracts`)**:
           - `packages/contracts/src/dto/webar/index.ts`:
             - `WEBAR_BUDGETS`: Enforces strict polygon and byte caps per jewellery type (Ring: 60k tris / 1.8MB; Earrings: 50k tris / 1.5MB; Pendant: 40k tris / 1.2MB; Necklace: 90k tris / 2.6MB; max texture 1024px).
             - `FitRuleSchema` & `FitRule`: Anchors proximal phalanx finger segment with scale and rotation offsets.
             - DTO read models for `WebarAssetReadModel`, `CreateHandoffRequest`, `CreateHandoffResponse`, and `RedeemHandoffResponse`.
        2. **Database Repository & Token Integrity (`packages/db`)**:
           - `packages/db/src/webar/webar-repository.ts`:
             - `validateWebarAssetBudgets()`: Enforces polygon, file size, and texture bounds.
             - `createWebarAsset()`, `findWebarAssetById()`, `findWebarAssetForProduct()`, `findWebarAssetForSetting()`.
             - `createArHandoffToken()`: Issues 10-minute expiry tokens storing only SHA-256 hash at rest.
             - `redeemArHandoffToken()`: Atomic one-time consumption (`consumed_at IS NULL`), rejecting expired (`AR_HANDOFF_INVALID`) and replayed (`AR_HANDOFF_REPLAYED` / `IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT`) attempts.
           - Tests: 7/7 passing in `packages/db/src/webar/webar-repository.spec.ts`.
        3. **HTTP API Handlers (`apps/web/src/api`)**:
           - `apps/web/src/api/webar-handlers.ts`:
             - `GET /api/v1/webar/{assetId}`: Retrieves asset specification and CDN URLs.
             - `POST /api/v1/ar/handoff`: Creates QR handoff token.
             - `GET /api/v1/ar/handoff/{token}`: Redeems token and issues session.
           - Routes registered in `apps/web/src/api/routes.ts` and `apps/web/src/api/index.ts`.
           - Tests: 5/5 passing in `apps/web/src/api/webar.spec.ts`.
        4. **Isolated 3D Viewer & Fallback Matrix (`apps/web/src/components/webar`)**:
           - `ThreeDViewer.tsx`: WebGL2 rendering simulation with OrbitControls (drag to rotate, pinch/wheel to zoom, reset view), accessible ARIA labels, and graceful fallbacks when WebGL2 is absent.
           - `WebArModal.tsx`:
             - **Strict On-Demand Camera Privacy Boundary (NFR-06)**: Camera access is NEVER requested on page load or on 3D viewer open; triggered strictly on explicit click.
             - **Zero Frame Upload**: Video frames processed in memory; zero bytes sent over the wire. MediaStream tracks cleanly terminated (`track.stop()`) on exit/unmount.
             - **Fallback Matrix**: Permission denied $\to$ static 3D viewer; Hand lost $>3\text{s}$ $\to$ repositioning prompt; Desktop $\to$ QR handoff generation.
           - `WebArLauncher.tsx`: Dynamic client island wrapper ensuring the WebAR bundle does not regress storefront bundle budgets (NFR-02).
        5. **Storefront & Product Detail Integration**:
           - `apps/web/src/app/(storefront)/ar/[assetId]/page.tsx`: Dedicated full-screen AR route.
           - `apps/web/src/app/(storefront)/ar/handoff/[handoffToken]/page.tsx`: Redeems single-use handoff token and redirects to `/ar/{assetId}` or displays honest expiration.
           - `apps/web/src/components/catalog/ProductDetailContent.tsx`: Integrated 3D/AR action when an approved model exists (omitted honestly when absent).
      - **Verification**:
        - `pnpm -r run typecheck`: 0 errors across all 10 packages.
        - `pnpm test`: 422 tests passing across monorepo (392 web tests, 15 db test suites).
        - Next.js production build (`next build --webpack`): 100% successful.
        - Initial JS bundle budget check (`pnpm --filter @maihan/web run check:budgets`): PASSED (Category 136.6 KB $\le$ 170 KB, Product 140.2 KB $\le$ 190 KB).
        - Commits `5e43fd4`, `882f47a`, `3aaa95b` pushed to repository.
      
      # PHASE 10 — WHATSAPP CHANNEL ADAPTER & STOREFRONT AI CONCIERGE INTEGRATION COMPLETE (2026-10-11)
      
      - **Objective**: Implement authoritative Phase 10 — WhatsApp Channel Adapter scope per `INITIAL_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` §17 and `technical_design/20_WHATSAPP_ARCHITECTURE.md`, verify shared brain tool registry parity with web, integrate accessible luxury AI Concierge client island into the storefront, and resolve gemstone detail presentation fallback.
      - **Architectural Deliverables**:
        1. **Phase 10 WhatsApp Channel Adapter (`packages/ai`, `packages/db`, `apps/web`)**:
           - `packages/contracts/src/dto/whatsapp/index.ts`: WhatsApp webhook payloads and template request schemas.
           - `packages/ai/src/whatsapp/`:
             - `types.ts`: `ChannelCapabilities` (`WHATSAPP_CAPABILITIES` non-streaming, 4096-char cap vs `WEB_CAPABILITIES`).
             - `formatter.ts`: `formatForWhatsApp()` formatting and `splitWhatsAppMessage()` 4000-char chunking with pagination markers.
             - `tools.ts`: `toolsFor('WEB')` and `toolsFor('WHATSAPP')` resolving to identical 10 controlled tools. `assertSharedBrainToolParity()` asserts 10 tools and identical structural mutation safety across channels.
           - `packages/db/src/whatsapp/whatsapp-repository.ts`:
             - Direct SQL idempotency on `(provider, external_event_id)` against `webhook_receipts`.
             - MSISDN normalization and customer lookup.
             - Conversation resolution by thread key and `channel='WHATSAPP'`.
             - Explicit `marketing_opt_in` consent checks.
           - `apps/web/src/api/whatsapp-handlers.ts`:
             - `GET /api/v1/webhooks/whatsapp`: Meta webhook hub.challenge verification.
             - `POST /api/v1/webhooks/whatsapp`: Raw body HMAC signature verification (`X-Hub-Signature-256`), deduplication, AI Orchestrator execution with `surface='whatsapp'`, response chunking and interaction logging.
             - `POST /api/v1/whatsapp/send-template`: Enforces marketing opt-in consent (403 when not opted in).
           - Test suite: 10/10 tests passing in `apps/web/src/api/whatsapp.spec.ts`.
        2. **Storefront AI Concierge Client Island (`apps/web`)**:
           - `apps/web/src/components/ai/AiConciergeDrawer.tsx`: Accessible luxury client island (`role="dialog"`, `aria-modal="true"`, focus trap, Escape key handling, WCAG 2.2 AA compliant).
           - Live SSE streaming client consuming `/api/v1/ai/chat` (`stream: true`), parsing typed events (`delta`, `citation`, `response`), rendering interactive suggestion chips and grounded references.
           - Styled with Swiss Private Banking & Dubai luxury theme tokens in `theme-styles.ts`.
           - Integrated into `StorefrontLayout.tsx`.
        3. **Product Detail & Gemstone Presentation Experience**:
           - `apps/web/src/lib/gemstone-adapter.ts`: Direct adapter bridging inventory `GemstoneDetailReadModel` to `ProductDetailReadModel` preserving all gemmological specifications, origins, cuts, and exact provenance evidence.
           - `apps/web/src/lib/catalog-server.ts`: Exported `loadGemstoneDetail()` data seam.
           - `apps/web/src/app/(storefront)/products/[productSlug]/page.tsx`: Resolves published product detail or falls back cleanly to gemstone detail (e.g. `K1324`, `PR5681`), rendering rich luxury detail views with zero 404s for verified loose stone inventory.
      - **Verification**:
        - `pnpm -r run typecheck`: 0 errors across all workspace packages.
        - `pnpm test`: Monorepo test suite clean (382 web tests passing).
        - Next.js production build (`next build --webpack`): 100% successful.
        - Commits `4088d49`, `b79c028`, `4eacb5e` pushed to `origin/main`.
      
      # PHASE 9 — AI CONCIERGE & ORCHESTRATION COMPLETE (2026-10-11)
      
      
      - **Objective**: Implement authoritative Phase 9 — AI Concierge & Orchestration scope per `INITIAL_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` §16 and `technical_design/18_AI_ORCHESTRATION_ARCHITECTURE.md`.
      - **Architectural Deliverables**:
        1. **New Workspace Package (`@maihan/ai`)**:

      # PHASE 8 — CERTIFICATE INTELLIGENCE COMPLETE (2026-10-11)
      
      - **Objective**: Implement authoritative Phase 8 — Certificate Intelligence scope per `INITIAL_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` §15 and `technical_design/17_CERTIFICATE_INTELLIGENCE.md`.
      - **Architectural Deliverables**:
        1. **Domain Layer (`@maihan/domain/certificate`)**:
           - `types.ts`: Domain models for `VerificationStatus` (`UNVERIFIED`, `EXTRACTED`, `CROSS_CHECKED`, `VERIFIED`, `REJECTED`), `DocumentKind`, `AnomalyType`, `ExtractedField<T>`, `CertificateExtractionSchema`, `StructuredGemstoneData`.
           - `confidence-policy.ts`: Strict confidence thresholds ($High \ge 0.90$, $Medium \in [0.70, 0.89]$, $Low \in [0.50, 0.69]$, $Rejected < 0.50$). Material field assessment (`weight_ct`, `origin`, `treatments`, `lab_name`). Whole-document verification requires $\ge 0.90$ across all material fields plus human review approval.
           - `cross-checks.ts`: 9 authoritative cross-checks:
             - `WEIGHT_MISMATCH_SLUG`: slug vs extracted weight difference $> 0.02$ ct.
             - `WEIGHT_MISMATCH_SUM`: pair sum mismatch $> 0.05$ ct.
             - `DIMENSION_IMPOSSIBLE`: dimensions $\le 0$ or single dimension $> 100$ mm.
             - `DIMENSION_MISMATCH`: extracted vs catalog dimension divergence $> 0.2$ mm.
             - `ORIGIN_CONFLICT`: conflicting origin classifications.
             - `TREATMENT_CONFLICT`: conflicting enhancement declarations.
             - `NUMBER_MISMATCH`: certificate number divergence.
             - `LOW_CONFIDENCE`: critical material field confidence $< 0.70$.
             - `MULTIPLE_STONES_DETECTED`: mismatch between stone count and certificate layout.
           - `state-machine.ts`: Deterministic transitions `transitionCertificateStatus()`. Prevents auto-promotion when confidence is insufficient or unresolved anomalies exist.
           - 18 unit tests in `packages/domain/src/certificate/certificate.spec.ts`.
        2. **Database Layer (`@maihan/db/certificate`)**:
           - `certificate-repository.ts`: Complete queries for `certificates`, `certificate_ocr_runs`, `certificate_extractions` with bounding boxes, `certificate_anomalies`, `verification_events` append-only audit trail, and `certificate_reviews`.
           - `getPublicCertificatesForGemstone()` querying authoritative `certificate_public` view (enforcing Invariant `INV-07`: confidence $< 0.50$ withheld, non-VERIFIED never rendered as verified truth).
        3. **HTTP API Layer (`apps/web/src/api`)**:
           - Routes registered under `/api/v1/certificates` and public view endpoints:
             - `POST /api/v1/certificates`: Register unverified certificate and obtain presigned upload URL.
             - `GET /api/v1/certificates/{certificateId}`: Retrieve document metadata.
             - `POST /api/v1/certificates/{certificateId}/ocr`: Process OCR extraction, execute 9 cross-checks, record anomalies and audit events.
             - `POST /api/v1/certificates/{certificateId}/review`: Gemmologist review with override support and 0.90 threshold enforcement (rejects with 422 UNPROCESSABLE on insufficient confidence).
             - `GET /api/v1/gemstones/{gemRef}/certificates/public`: Public read-model view strictly enforcing Invariant INV-07.
           - 6 integration tests in `apps/web/src/api/certificate.spec.ts`.
      - **Verification**:
        - `pnpm -r run typecheck`: 0 errors across all 8 workspace packages.
        - `pnpm test`: 687 tests passing across entire monorepo (`@maihan/domain`: 227, `@maihan/contracts`: 13, `@maihan/db`: 81, `tools/migrate`: 5, `@maihan/web`: 367).

      | Task 11 — Refund Request & Multi-stage Approval Workflow | `dd383e3` | **Verified Complete** | Refund requests in `refunds` table, bounds enforcement (`amount <= captured_amount`), state transitions (`REQUESTED` -> `APPROVED` -> `SUCCEEDED` / `REJECTED`), order and payment status synchronization. 3 tests (`refunds.spec.ts`, `refund.spec.ts`). |
      | Task 12 — Reservation Expiry Sweeper & Stock Release | `bcf94ab` | **Verified Complete** | Expired reservation sweeper (`sweepExpiredReservations`) canceling uncompleted sessions older than 30 minutes, releasing inventory locks, emitting `InventoryReleased` in `domain_events`. 2 tests (`reservation-sweeper.spec.ts`, `reservation-expiry.spec.ts`). |
      | Task 13 — Pure Order State Machine & Timeline | `51477fa` | **Verified Complete** | Pure deterministic state transition function `transitionOrder(currentState, event)` in `@maihan/domain`, `order_events` timeline tracking, audit trail logging, cancel endpoint, immutable terminal state protection. 11 tests (`order.spec.ts`, `order-events.spec.ts`). |
      
      ## Architecture & Commerce Invariants Verification
      - **Price Authority**: Client is never authoritative; client prices mismatched with database prices reject with `409 PRICE_CHANGED`.
      - **Atomic Stock Reservation**: Stock is reserved inside PostgreSQL transaction with 30-minute expiration. Pairs reserve atomically or fail with `PAIR_RESERVATION_FAILED`.
      - **Order Immutability**: Placed orders store immutable snapshots of pricing, gemstones, and settings. Changes are append-only in `order_events` and `audit_logs`.
      - **Payment Security**: Webhook HMAC verified over raw body bytes before JSON parsing using constant-time comparison.
      - **Authorization Boundary**: Strict customer isolation. Accessing another customer's cart, order, pr

784:## 19. PHASE 12 - ADMIN + CMS
## 19. PHASE 12 - ADMIN + CMS

**Objective.** Deliver the structured CMS with a review workflow, and the back office needed to operate the catalogue, trust and AI surfaces.

**Dependencies.** Phases 2, 3, 7, 8. Provides the review surfaces Phases 8 and 9 depend on, so it may need a thin earlier slice.

**Inputs.** `pages`, `content_blocks`, `navigations`, `redirects`, `seo_metadata`; RBAC matrix; audit requirements; migration console requirements.

**Outputs.** CMS with 20 typed block renderers and approval workflow; admin modules for products, gemstones, settings, taxonomy, inventory, orders, customers, DYO, certificates, content, media, AI, migration, operations; RBAC; audit trail; derived-reference approval queue.

**Epics.**
- E12.1 CMS blocks and editing
- E12.2 Approval workflow
- E12.3 Navigation, redirects and SEO
- E12.4 Catalogue administration
- E12.5 Trust review console
- E12.6 Migration console and conflict resolution
- E12.7 AI operations console
- E12.8 RBAC and audit

**Tasks.**
1. Implement typed blocks and one renderer per `content_block_type`.
2. Implement page revisions and the DRAFT, IN_REVIEW, PUBLISHED, ARCHIVED workflow.
3. Require second approval for trust-bearing content.
4. Build navigation editing and redirect management with bulk import of the 44,218 rows.
5. Implement SEO metadata management.
6. Build catalogue admin with bulk edit, preview and undo.
7. Build the derived-reference approval queue for the 221 and any future inferred identifiers.
8. Build the migration console exposing batches, counts, conflicts and quarantined rows.
9. Build the conflict resolution UI that never silently overwrites.
10. Build the certificate review queue and anomaly triage.
11. Build the AI console: conversations, tool-call audit, guardrail events, evaluation runs.
12. Implement RBAC across the ten roles with `BYPASSRLS` for admin and full audit.
13. Implement bulk operations with preview, approval, audit and bounded undo.

**Acceptance criteria.** Approval workflow functional with two-person rule on trust content. Every privileged mutation writes an audit row with before and after state. The 221 can be individually approved or rejected. Conflicts are visible and resolvable without data loss. Navigation is data-driven.

**Tests required.** RBAC matrix tests across all ten roles for every admin endpoint; approval-workflow tests; audit completeness tests; bulk-operation undo tests; conflict-resolution tests; block-renderer snapshot tests.

**Definition of Done.** The catalogue, trust and AI surfaces are operable by humans with full attribution.

**What must NOT be implemented yet.** No CRM or ERP integration. No AI-generated content published without human approval. No destructive bulk action without typed confirmation. Do not grant `BYPASSRLS` outside the admin role.

---

## 20. PHASE 13 - PERFORMANCE + SECURITY + OBSERVABILITY

-- One logical unit: CMS tables.

CREATE TABLE pages (
  id            UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
  slug          TEXT NOT NULL UNIQUE,
  title         TEXT NOT NULL,
  page_kind     TEXT NOT NULL,      -- STATIC | EDUCATION | BLOG | POLICY | LANDING | SHOWROOM
  status        TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'DRAFT' CHECK (status IN ('DRAFT','PUBLISHED','ARCHIVED')),
  blocks        JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT '[]'::jsonb,   -- typed ContentBlock array
  raw_markdown  TEXT,
  hero_asset_id UUID REFERENCES assets(id),
  template      TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'default',
  published_at  TIMESTAMPTZ,
  created_at    TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
  updated_at    TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
  deleted_at    TIMESTAMPTZ,
  version       INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 1
);
--> statement-breakpoint
CREATE UNIQUE INDEX ON pages (slug) WHERE deleted_at IS NULL;
--> statement-breakpoint
CREATE INDEX ON pages (page_kind, status) WHERE deleted_at IS NULL;
--> statement-breakpoint
CREATE INDEX ON pages USING gin (blocks jsonb_path_ops);
--> statement-breakpoint
CREATE INDEX ON pages USING gin (to_tsvector('english', coalesce(title,'') || ' ' || coalesce(raw_markdown,'')));
--> statement-breakpoint
CREATE TABLE page_revisions (
  id           UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
  page_id      UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES pages(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  version      INTEGER NOT NULL,
  blocks       JSONB NOT NULL,
  raw_markdown TEXT,
  editor_id    UUID,
  note         TEXT,
  created_at   TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
  UNIQUE (page_id, version)
);
--> statement-breakpoint
CREATE TABLE navigations (
  id         UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
  key        TEXT NOT NULL,      -- 'header','footer','mega_menu','mobile'
  label      TEXT NOT NULL,
  items      JSONB NOT NULL,     -- nested [{label,href,children,variety_id,external}]
  locale     TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'en',
  updated_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
  UNIQUE (key, locale)
);
--> statement-breakpoint
CREATE TABLE redirects (
  id          UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
  from_path   TEXT NOT NULL UNIQUE,
  to_path     TEXT NOT NULL,
  status_code SMALLINT NOT NULL DEFAULT 301,
  reason      TEXT,
  created_at  TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now()
);
--> statement-breakpoint
CREATE INDEX ON redirects (from_path);
--> statement-breakpoint
CREATE TABLE seo_metadata (
  id               UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
  entity_kind      seo_entity_kind NOT NULL,
  entity_id        UUID NOT NULL,
  title            TEXT,
  description      TEXT,
  canonical_path   TEXT,
  og_image_url     TEXT,
  robots           TEXT,
  noindex          BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT false,
  structured_extra JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'::jsonb,
  updated_at       TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
  UNIQUE (entity_kind, entity_id)
);

```

- path: technical_design/23_ADMIN_CONSOLE_AND_RBAC.md
  content: |
      
      The volume asymmetry drives the UI: 19,665 stones, 10,349 variants, 9,812 DYO configurations and 44,218 URLs mean every editing screen is a **filterable, virtualised, server-paginated list** with bulk actions. A paginated table is not viable at this scale.
      
      ## 3. Authorization
      
      RBAC with resource-level scoping. `[INFERRED / PROPOSED]`; the source is `[UNKNOWN]`.
      
      | Role | Scope |
      |---|---|
      | `ADMIN_SUPER` | everything |
      | `CATALOG_MANAGER` | products, gemstones, settings, taxonomy, media |
      | `CONTENT_EDITOR` | content, navigation, SEO |
      | `CONTENT_PUBLISHER` | content plus approval |
      | `COMMERCE_OPERATOR` | orders, refunds, fulfilment, inventory |
      | `TRUST_REVIEWER` | certificates, anomalies |
      | `CONCIERGE_AGENT` | assigned conversations, customer read |
      | `AI_OPERATIONS` | conversations, tool calls, evaluations, guardrails |
      | `MIGRATION_ENGINEER` | import batches, conflicts, derived approvals |
      | `READ_ONLY_ANALYST` | dashboards only, no PII |
      
      `READ_ONLY_ANALYST` being PII-free by role rather than by UI hiding is deliberate. A dashboard that merely hides a column is one query away from exposing a customer's address.
      
      ## 4. Enforcement
      
      | Layer | Mechanism |
      |---|---|
      | Policy | `policies/` per application service, written once and shared by API and admin |
      | Database | RLS via a `BYPASSRLS` role that only the trusted server role holds |
      | Application | Admin API handlers call the same services as customer-facing routes |
      | Audit | Every privileged action written to `audit_logs` with before and after state |
      
      The critical property: **the admin console has no privileged data path of its own.** It calls the same application services as the storefront. If a policy allows an admin action, it is a real policy, inspectable and testable, not an admin-only bypass.
      
      ## 5. AI assist in admin
      
      Draft copy, facet suggestions, and DYO compatibility rule drafting, all human-reviewed.
      
      | Rule | Detail |
      |---|---|
      | Provenance | Every proposal recorded with `provenance_kind='AI_SUGGESTED'` |
      | State | `review_state='PENDING'` |
      | Publication | An AI suggestion can **never** be published directly |
      | Audit | The approving human is recorded separately from the proposing model |
      
      `[OBSERVED]` `entities/gemstones/all_gemstones.json` is 11 KB of all-null objects. That file is the concrete argument against unattended AI extraction writing production rows: the platform already contains a cautionary example of an automated extraction that produced structurally valid, completely empty data.
      
      ## 6. Bulk operations
      
      | Rule | Detail |
      |---|---|
      | Preview | Every bulk edit previews the change set before applying |
      | Approval | Records who approved it |
      | Audit | One `audit_logs` row per affected entity |
      | Reversibility | Bounded window via `page_revisions`-style snapshots |
      | Destructive | Requires typed confirmation |
      
      At this scale bulk operations are the normal case, not an exception: 911 stones need a stone type, and `price_entries` derived from 1,024 observations need review. Both are bulk operations, so the safety machinery is on the critical path for the initial data load, not an edge case.
      
      ## 7. Concurrency and conflict handling
      
      | Concern | Approach |
      |---|---|
      | Concurrent edit | `version` column; 409 on mismatch with a diff view |
      | Bulk vs single edit | Bulk snapshot captures the pre-state; single edits during a bulk are detected and reported |
      | Long bulk operations | Job-backed with progress, cancellable, resumable |
      | Preview staleness | A preview carries a token bound to the source version; a changed source invalidates it |
      
      A stale preview applying to changed data is how bulk edits cause silent data loss, so the preview token is bound to the source version rather than trusted as a snapshot in time.
      
      ## 8. Validation rules
      
      1. Every admin mutation goes through an application service with a policy check.
      2. Roles are assigned server-side; the client never asserts a role.
      3. Bulk operations above a threshold require a second approver, matching the inventory rule in doc 15.
      4. Every privileged action writes `audit_logs` with before and after state.
      5. `AI_SUGGESTED` rows cannot reach PUBLISHED without a named human approver.
      6. Destructive operations require typed confirmation matching the target identifier.
      7. `READ_ONLY_ANALYST` responses never include PII, enforced server-side.
      
      ## 9. API
      
      Admin routes live under `/api/v1/admin/*` and mirror the module structure. Every route declares its required role in OpenAPI, and the generated client derives the UI's visible actions from that declaration, so the UI cannot offer an action the policy would deny.
      
      | Pattern | Purpose |
      |---|---|
      | `GET /admin/{resource}` | server-paginated, filterable, sort-whitelisted |
      | `GET /admin/{resource}/{id}/history` | audit trail for one entity |
      | `POST /admin/{resource}/bulk/preview` | returns a preview token and diff |
      | `POST /admin/{resource}/bulk/apply` | applies with the preview token |
      | `POST /admin/{resource}/bulk/revert` | within the bounded window |
      
      ## 10. Error handling
      
      | Code | Status | Meaning |
      |---|---|---|
      | `FORBIDDEN` | 403 | Role lacks the capability |
      | `VERSION_CONFLICT` | 409 | Concurrent edit; diff returned |
      | `PREVIEW_STALE` | 409 | Source changed since preview |
      | `DUAL_APPROVAL_REQUIRED` | 409 | Bulk operation above the threshold |
      | `AI_SUGGESTION_NOT_PUBLISHABLE` | 409 | Attempt to publish an unreviewed proposal |
      | `CONFIRMATION_MISMATCH` | 400 | Typed confirmation did not match |
      | `BULK_JOB_FAILED` | 202 with job id | Job-backed bulk failed; partial state reported per row |
      
      ## 11. Security boundaries
      
      - No admin-only database role for application code; one `BYPASSRLS` role, server-side only.
      - Session cookies are `HttpOnly`, `Secure`, `SameSite=Strict`, with step-up auth for destructive actions.
      - Admin access is IP-allowlisted where the network permits, and always MFA.
      - Audit rows are append-only; there is no delete path for `audit_logs`.
      - Analyst exports are PII-scrubbed server-side, not in the browser.
      - Bulk applies are rate-limited and capped per request to bound blast radius.
      
      ## 12. Observability
      
      Metrics: admin actions by role and module, authorization denials, bulk operation volume and failure rate, preview staleness rate, review queue depth per queue, time-to-approve for certificates and derived references. Alerts on a spike in denials (misconfiguration or credential compromise), on bulk failures, and on review queues not draining.
      
      ## 13. Testing requirements
      
      | Test | Proves |
      |---|---|---|
      | Policy matrix | Every role against every module matches the scope table |
      | Shared path | Admin and storefront calls hit the same service and the same policy |
      | RLS | Direct DB access as the application role cannot read across tenants or read `audit_logs` deletes |
      | Analyst PII | No PII field appears in any analyst payload |
      | AI suggestion gate | An `AI_SUGGESTED` row cannot publish without a human approver |
      | Bulk preview staleness | A source change invalidates the preview |
      | Audit completeness | Every privileged action writes before and after state |
      | Conflict | Concurrent edit yields 409 and a usable diff |
      | Typed confirmation | A destructive action cannot execute without exact confirmation |
      | Scale | List endpoints paginate and filter at 19,665 rows without full-table reads |
      
      [read_files: showing lines 38-167 of 190. Use code_search to locate the part you need and read a window around it, or call read_files again with offset=168 to continue.]

# PHASE 12 PRE-FLIGHT REPORT

## 1. Authoritative Phase 12 definition found in the repository

Confirmed from `INITIAL_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` §19 (line 784):

- **Name/scope match**: The repository's plan says **"Phase 12 — ADMIN + CMS"** with epics **E12.1–E12.8** exactly as your prompt describes. ✅ No phase-numbering discrepancy in the plan itself.
- **However** — one real discrepancy must be reported: `.opencode/memory/IMPLEMENTATION_STATE.md` labels the **observability** work (committed `1444dd9`, 2026-10-12) as "PHASE 12 — Production Observability", and `docs/api/API_INVENTORY_AND_CURL_GUIDE.md` already flags this. Per the plan, observability is **Phase 13**. The memory file mislabels the phase; the committed code is mislabelled in its report. The plan wins — this phase remains **Admin + CMS**, and I will not reinterpret it.

## 2. Current Git state

- Branch: `main`, HEAD `1444dd9`, 96 commits, backend bot-less (2 human contributors).
- **Working tree has uncommitted user changes** (preserved untouched):

```
M  .opencode/memory/IMPLEMENTATION_STATE.md, SKILL_MATRIX_AND_TOOLING.md,
   apps/web/{next.config.ts, package.json, src/app/layout.tsx}, pnpm-lock.yaml,
   technical_design/{02_TECH_STACK.md, 40_ARCHITECTURE_DECISION_RECORDS.md}
D  freebuff-chat-2026-10-08T17-56-46.813Z.md
?? apps/web/src/components/development/, docs/api/, scripts/
```

These look like they are part of the Agentation/observability work described in memory but possibly not fully committed. I will stage explicit paths only and never include these unless they belong to a Global Task I complete.

## 3. Dependency status (Phases 2, 3, 7, 8) — verified in code/data

| Dependency | Verified evidence | Status |
|---|---|---|
| Phase 2 (migration) | `import_batches` = 13 rows staged; `tools/migrate` runner committed; **`staging_conflicts` = 328 rows** | **READY** |
| Phase 3 (catalogue) | `products` 9,900 (all `DRAFT`, 0 `ACTIVE`); settings 964; vocabulary seeded; CatalogQueryService serving storefront | **READY** |
| Phase 7 (DYO) | `dyo_*` repos, state machine, compatibility engine, API + UI committed | **READY** |
| Phase 8 (certificates) | `certificates` = 1,135 rows; state machine, cross-checks, `certificate_public` view, review API | **READY** |
| Derived references | Query returned **8,876** rows with `source_sku IS NULL AND source_sku_derived IS NOT NULL AND sku_evidence_status='INFERRED'` — **NOT the 221 the plan names.** Needs reconciliation during Stage E before building the approval queue. | ⚠️ Investigate |
| R-17 thin slice (cert review UI for Phase 8) | Phase 8 shipped with HTTP review API; thin review-UI slice was to come from Phase 12 | In-scope for Stage E |

## 4. Existing reusable infrastructure (do not rebuild)

- **CMS DB tables exist** (`0012_cms.sql`): `pages` (JSONB `blocks`, `status DRAFT|PUBLISHED|ARCHIVED`), `page_revisions`, `navigations`, `redirects` (**44,218 rows already loaded**), `seo_metadata`. All empty except redirects.
- **20 content-block-type enum exists** in `0002_enums.sql` — 20 values, matching plan §19 exactly ✅
- **Audit**: `audit_logs` (148 rows) with before/after; append-only; used by commerce/order repos.
- **RLS**: `0016_rls.sql` — 6 policies; `app.staff` / `BYPASSRLS`-role pattern already present.
- **Error envelope + rate limiter + correlation ids**: `apps/web/src/api/{envelope,handlers,routes,ratelimit}`.
- **Cert review services**: verification state machine, 9 cross-checks, `certificate_reviews`, `verification_events`.
- **AI telemetry tables**: `conversations`, `tool_calls`, `guardrail_events`, `ai_interactions` (all currently 0 rows — console must show "no telemetry yet" honestly).
- **Present but thin**: `/admin/**` route is a null render placeholder; route manifest reserves it; no `policies/`, no admin API, no CMS repo/service, no CMS contracts in `packages/contracts/dto`.

## 5. Storefront / catalogue / AI smoke tests (executed just now)

| Check | Command | Result |
|---|---|---|
| Storefront live | `curl localhost:3000/` | 200 ✅ |
| Catalogue from backend | homepage loads settings via `catalogServer().listSettings`, DB queried directly: settings 964 present | ✅ |
| Redirects present | `SELECT count(*) FROM redirects` | 44,218 ✅ |
| AI structural no-write boundary | `assertStructuralMutationSafety()` in `packages/ai` registry + eval suites | ✅ present |
| Public page visibility rule | DRAFT products correctly not published (products all DRAFT by design, storefront gates on `ACTIVE`) | ✅ as designed |

## 6. Blockers / discrepancies to resolve

1. **The 221 vs 8,876 discrepancy** on derived references — must be reconciled against the migration evidence before building E12.6's queue (may be a naming/columns difference rather than a count error).
2. **No authentication layer exists yet** — memory says auth/RBAC is Phase 13-adjacent per doc 23 §3 `INFERRED/PROPOSED`. Phase 12 E12.8 requires the ten-role matrix enforced server-side. I'll build the policy layer per doc 23 but need to confirm the identity/session source (currently `x-customer-id` header seam only).

## 7. First authorized Global Task

**GT-1 (E12.8 foundation first, per Stage A):** Traceability matrix + ten-role RBAC policy module (`policies/` per doc 23 §3–4) on the existing `audit_logs` and envelope, with the full policy-matrix test suite. Acceptance: every role-module pair tested against doc 23's scope table; `FORBIDDEN` envelope; no PII to `READ_ONLY_ANALYST`; per §16 one focused commit.

**Question:** The plan says 221 derived-only DYO identities; the DB has 8,876 products with inferred derived SKUs. How do I proceed on E12.6's approval queue?
_Answer:_ Investigate then proceed

**Question:** Your working tree has uncommitted changes (Agentation overlay, next.config, docs, etc.). How should I handle them while making per-task commits?
_Answer:_ Leave untouched, commit mine only

133:**Example B - SKU identity for the 221 records with no `product.sku`**
135:- `[OBSERVED]` 221 records have `sku = ""` in `crawler.sqlite` and `name = "JavaScript is disabled"`. `[VERIFIED]` all 221 are HTTP 502 responses of 121-2,528 bytes, so no attribute data exists for them. `[VERIFIED]` **0 of the 221 have any row in `specifications.jsonl`**, therefore `specifications.item_id` is absent for all 221 and cannot be used to establish identity.
138:- `[VERIFIED]` All 221 slugs parse, yielding **221 distinct derived SKUs**.
139:- `[VERIFIED]` **All 221 derived SKUs are new. Zero collide with the observed 1,024.** They are therefore 221 additional distinct DYO products whose HTML fetch failed, not duplicate records. `[VERIFIED]` the 1,245 product URLs are all distinct.
141:  1. all 8 stone references appearing in the 221 (`K1161`, `K1173`, `K1175`, `K2198`, `K2199`, `K2209`, `K2478`, `K506`) also appear as distinct `K` references in the loose-stone URL inventory of 19,665 refs;
142:  2. all 168 distinct `(design, metal)` pairs in the 221 exist in the settings catalog of 10,349 pairs.
143:  Of the 8 stones, 5 (`K1161`, `K1173`, `K1175`, `K2478`, `K506`) also appear among the 35 stones referenced by the observed 1,024; **3 (`K2198`, `K2199`, `K2209`) are new stone identities** present only in this failed-fetch set. `[VERIFIED]` the 221 span 24 distinct setting cores.
144:- **Conclusion, and the evidence label.** Identity for the 221 is **`[INFERRED / PROPOSED]` with two-source corroboration** - not OBSERVED. The package rule *"do not manufacture SKU from URL strings"* is respected: no value is invented. A parser with measured precision of 1,024/1,024 is applied, and the result is stored in `source_sku_derived` with `sku_evidence_status = 'INFERRED'`, never in `source_sku`, and never promoted to a confirmed identifier without human review (INV-08). Attribute values (weight, dimensions, colour, treatment, price) for these 221 remain **`[UNKNOWN]`** and must be null until a re-fetch supplies them. They must not be inferred from the slug beyond the SKU token itself, nor inferred from a sibling product sharing the same stone.
90:| V18 | All 221 failed-fetch DYO rows land with `sku_evidence_status='INFERRED'`, `source_sku IS NULL`, `state='DRAFT'`, and every attribute column null | Halt |

# 🛑 CRITICAL PRE-FLIGHT FINDING — MIGRATION GATE V18 IS VIOLATED

Before implementing anything in Stage E, this must be reported. **All 8,876 derived rows violate the locked evidence rules**, meaning **Phase 2's blocking exit gate (V18) has NOT actually passed**, and Phase 12's derived-reference approval queue (E12.6) is built on top of a broken premise.

## Evidence (executed read-only against dev DB)

| Check result | Value | What the locked docs require |
|---|---|---|
| Rows with `source_sku NULL AND source_sku_derived NOT NULL AND sku_evidence_status='INFERRED'` | **8,876** | **221 exactly** (SoT §133–144, td33 §5, plan §53/§1023) |
| `kind` breakdown | CONFIGURED 8,788 + READY_MADE 88 | Derived identity should apply only to the 221 failed-fetch DYO rows; READY_MADE has observed SKUs |
| `total_weight_ct / jewellery_type / price_amount` set on derived rows | **All 8,876 have attribute values populated** (0 with `jewellery_type NULL`) — e.g. `total_weight_ct=1.300`, `jewellery_type=EARRINGS`, `price_amount=12995` for every derived row | V18: **every attribute column must be NULL** on the 221 |
| Sample slug | `alexandrite-earrings-oval-1.30ct-14k-white-gold-js114w14-pr12995` | The `pr12995` price token is a slug token — **made-up price "12995"** derived from URL, not observed |
| `ready_made` rows using `source_sku_derived` | 88 (`J10763`, `J11227`, …) | READY_MADE SKUs were observed in the 1,024; they should never be inferred-only |
| Gates per td33 | `V18–V20 = **Halt**` level (blocking) | Migration should have halted before this state |

## What this means per `PROJECT_SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md` §6/§7 and td33

1. This is exactly the failure mode the document names at line "~these rows are the practical test of the whole evidence system... A pipeline that 'helpfully' filled in their attributes would look successful and be quietly wrong, which is the specific failure mode every evidence label exists to prevent." The migration **"helpfully" parsed attributes out of the slug** (weights like `1.30ct`, jewellery type `earrings`, price token `pr12995`) — manufacturing observed-style values from URL strings, which is explicitly prohibited by the archive rule `do not manufacture SKU from URL strings` and the V18 gate.
2. The 8,788-vs-221 count inflation makes it likely that **the migration also inferred `source_sku_derived` for rows that had real observed SKUs** (they end up inferred-only because their observed identity got dropped), plus all 88 READY_MADE rows misclassified.

## Impact on Phase 12

- **E12.6 (milestone/reliance)**: The "221 approval queue" cannot be built correctly on top of 8,876 misclassified rows. Building approval UI that treats these as legitimate `INFERRED`-evidence rows would formally ratify the fabricated attribute values and violate **INV-08** at scale.
- **Phases 2's exit gate → not passed** → the root cause lives in `tools/migrate` + Phase 2 extraction stages, **outside Phase 12's authorized scope** per your instruction: *"Fix only what is explicitly in Phase 12 scope... Do not silently expand the phase."*
- What Phase 12 **can** still safely do: since the data defect is in the migration layer, and per the doc's mandate that conflicts are visible and not silently resolved, I propose Phase 12's migration console first surfaces this as the top-priority quarantine/conflict class it is.

## Your options (this needs an explicit decision — it is the "significant destructive/hard-to-undo effect" tied to another phase's gate)

**Question:** The migration violated blocking gate V18: 8,876 (not 221) rows are inferred-only AND every one has attribute values parsed from URL slugs (weight/jewellery type/price), which the locked docs explicitly forbid for the 221 failed-fetch rows. How should I proceed?
_Answer:_ Fix migration first

> _Reasoning_
> The user chose: Fix migration first. That means fixing the Phase 2 migration (tools/migrate + extraction) so derived rows satisfy V18 — the 221 failed-fetch rows get NULL attributes, and rows with observed SKUs regain them — before Phase 12 Stage E. This is significant and needs care. Let me investigate the migration code to find the root cause.

/**
 * tools/migrate — the ordered stage registry (plan task 10; doc 33 "Phasing"
 * M1-M9; plan tasks 11-20 as DECLARED future work, not implementation).
 *
 * Requirements held here:
 *   - stage identities are explicit and stable (ALPHABET-free constants);
 *   - dependency order is the registry order: a failed stage stops every
 *     dependent stage downstream;

 *   - future catalogue stages are DECLARED (empty definitions with their

}

/**
 * tools/migrate — stage "derived-identities" (Phase 2 Global Task 15 = plan
 * detailed task 13, epic E2.4 continuation).
 *
 * Plan task 15 (verbatim): "Implement the 221 derived-identity write path"
 * — gate V18 green (source_sku NULL, source_sku_derived set, INFERRED,
 * DRAFT, null attributes), the V19 regression, V20 = 0 collisions.
 *
 * The 221 are the DYO pages whose fetch failed (crawler.sqlite sku = '',
 * HTTP 502 shells; Example B, SoT §3.1): identity is [INFERRED / PROPOSED]
 * with two-source corroboration. Enforced here, verbatim:
 *
 *   - source_sku            = NULL (never a parse; never manufactured)
 *   - source_sku_derived    = the grammar-1 parsed identity
 *   - sku_evidence_status   = 'INFERRED'
 *   - state                 = 'DRAFT' (never published; INV-08's human gate)
 *   - attribute columns     = NULL (total_weight_ct, colour_id, variety_id;
 *     the stone measures live on the referenced gemstone rows, never here)
 *
 * NOT inferred from the slug beyond the SKU token itself (Example B): no
 * weight, dimension, colour or treatment value is derived for these rows.
 * Composition links (setting variant + stone ref) are the corroborated
 * identity that INV-01 requires to exist at all.
 *
 * Gates (all SQL, executed at the end of the stage):
 *   V18 — all 221 rows carry the exact shape above; halts otherwise.
 *   V19 — the derived-SKU composition reproduces all 1,024 observed SKUs
 *         (the parser regression, re-proven on the corpus each run).
 *   V20 — zero derived/observed SKU collisions; any collision quarantines.
 *
 * Idempotency: enforcement UPDATEs carry WHERE-guards so a full rerun
 * matches zero rows (V17 semantics); the corpus parse is pure.
 */
import { createHash } from 'node:crypto';
import { readFileSync } from 'node:fs';

import type { StageContext } from './context.js';

export const EXPECTED_DERIVED_ROWS = 221;
export const EXPECTED_OBSERVED_SKUS = 1_024;

export interface DerivedStageResult {
  readonly derivedRows: number;
  readonly enforced: number; // rows the guards actually touched (first run)
  readonly observedParsed: number;
  readonly collisions: number;
}

/** The products.jsonl records with an EMPTY sku — the 221 failed-fetch set. */
export function failedFetchUrls(path: string, expectDigest?: string): readonly string[] {
  if (expectDigest !== undefined) {
    const actual = createHash('sha256').update(readFileSync(path)).digest('hex');
    if (actual !== expectDigest) {
      throw new Error(`derived-identities: products.jsonl digest mismatch: expected ${expectDigest}, actual ${actual}`);
    }
  }
  const urls: string[] = [];
  for (const line of readFileSync(path, 'utf8').split('\n')) {
    if (line.trim().length === 0) continue;
    const r = JSON.parse(line) as { url: string; sku: string };
    if (r.sku.length === 0) urls.push(r.url);
  }
  return Object.freeze(urls);
}

/**
 * Execute the derived-identities stage over the canonical catalogue.
 * `corpus` (default true) holds THE 221-row count expectation to the real
 * products.jsonl corpus; disposable-fixture runs pass corpus:false and only
 * verify structure (each fixture row still gets the full shape enforced).
 */
export async function runDerivedIdentities(ctx: StageContext, options?: { corpus?: boolean; productsDigest?: string }): Promise<DerivedStageResult> {
  const db = ctx.db;

  // 1. the failed-fetch set (exactly 221; any other count is a spine defect).
  const urls = failedFetchUrls(ctx.artifactPath('products.jsonl'), options?.productsDigest);
  if (options?.corpus !== false && urls.length !== EXPECTED_DERIVED_ROWS) {
    throw new Error(`derived-identities: expected exactly ${EXPECTED_DERIVED_ROWS} failed-fetch rows, found ${urls.length}`);
  }
  const distinctUrls = new Set(urls);

  if (distinctUrls.size !== urls.length) {
    throw new Error(`derived-identities: duplicate failed-fetch URLs (${urls.length} rows, ${distinctUrls.size} distinct)`);
  }

  // 2. every one must have a canonical CONFIGURED product row (the derived
  //    identity class of the canonical stage); find them from staging.
  const staged = await db.query<{ source_url: string; design_code: string | null; gem_ref_derived: string | null }>(
    `SELECT source_url, design_code, gem_ref_derived FROM staging_parsed_urls WHERE url_family = 'DYO' AND source_url = ANY($1::text[]) ORDER BY source_url`,
    [urls],
  );
  if (staged.rows.length !== urls.length) {
    throw new Error(`derived-identities: ${String(urls.length - staged.rows.length)} failed-fetch URLs have no staging parse row`);
  }
  // Deterministic pairing: the STAGING rows arrive sorted by source_url; keep
  // the pair arrays in the same order so UNNEST($2)/UNNEST($3) stay aligned.
  for (const row of staged.rows) {
    if (row.design_code === null || row.gem_ref_derived === null) {
      throw new Error(`derived-identities: a failed-fetch URL's parse lacks the design/ref tokens (${String(row.design_code)}/${String(row.gem_ref_derived)})`);
    }
  }

  // 3. update the canonical rows with WHERE guards (idempotent, deep:
  //    enforced rows don't match a second time). UNNEST keeps the URL ->
  //    identity alignment by index.
  const orderedUrls = staged.rows.map((row) => row.source_url);
  const values = staged.rows.map((row) => `${String(row.design_code)}-${String(row.gem_ref_derived)}`.toUpperCase());

  const update = await db.query(
    `UPDATE products p
        SET source_sku = NULL,
            source_sku_derived = d.identity,
            sku_evidence_status = 'INFERRED',
            state = 'DRAFT',
            total_weight_ct = NULL,
            colour_id = NULL,
            variety_id = NULL
       FROM (SELECT UNNEST($1::text[]) AS source_url, UNNEST($2::text[]) AS identity) d
      WHERE p.source_url = d.source_url
        AND p.kind = 'CONFIGURED'
        AND (p.source_sku IS NOT NULL
          OR p.source_sku_derived IS DISTINCT FROM d.identity
          OR p.sku_evidence_status <> 'INFERRED'
          OR p.state <> 'DRAFT'
          OR p.total_weight_ct IS NOT NULL
          OR p.colour_id IS NOT NULL
          OR p.variety_id IS NOT NULL)`,
    [orderedUrls, values],
  );
  const enforced = update.rowCount ?? 0;

  // 4. V18 — the exact 221 rows must now carry the required shape.
  const v18 = await db.query<{ violations: string }>(
    `SELECT count(*)::text AS violations FROM products
      WHERE source_url = ANY($1::text[])
        AND (source_sku IS NOT NULL
          OR source_sku_derived IS NULL
          OR sku_evidence_status <> 'INFERRED'
          OR state <> 'DRAFT'
          OR total_weight_ct IS NOT NULL
          OR colour_id IS NOT NULL
          OR variety_id IS NOT NULL
          OR kind <> 'CONFIGURED')`,
    [urls],
  );
  if (Number(v18.rows[0]?.violations ?? '0') !== 0) {
    throw new Error(`derived-identities: gate V18 FAILED — violations ${String(v18.rows[0]?.violations)}`);
  }

  // 5. V19 regression: the derived-identity composition reproduces every
  //    observed SKU of the corpus (the parse each run; no test corpus drift).
  const observed = await db.query<{ n: string }>(
    `SELECT count(*)::text AS n FROM products WHERE kind = 'CONFIGURED' AND sku_evidence_status = 'OBSERVED' AND source_sku IS NOT NULL AND source_sku_derived IS NULL`,
  );
  if (options?.corpus !== false && Number(observed.rows[0]?.n ?? '0') !== EXPECTED_OBSERVED_SKUS) {
    throw new Error(`derived-identities: gate V19 regression FAILED — observed SKU rows ${String(observed.rows[0]?.n)} != ${EXPECTED_OBSERVED_SKUS}`);
  }

  // 6. V20 — zero collisions between derived and observed identities.
  const collisions = await db.query<{ n: string }>(
    `SELECT count(*)::text AS n FROM products d
      WHERE d.sku_evidence_status = 'INFERRED' AND d.source_sku_derived IS NOT NULL
        AND EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM products o WHERE o.sku_evidence_status = 'OBSERVED' AND o.source_sku = d.source_sku_derived)`,
  );
  const collisionCount = Number(collisions.rows[0]?.n ?? '0');
  if (collisionCount !== 0) {
    throw new Error(`derived-identities: gate V20 FAILED — ${collisionCount} derived/observed SKU collisions (quarantine required, never merge)`);
  }

  return { derivedRows: urls.length, enforced, observedParsed: EXPECTED_OBSERVED_SKUS, collisions: collisionCount };
}

  readonly jewelleryType: string;
  readonly varietySlug: string | null;
  readonly colourSlug: string | null;

  readonly skuEvidenceStatus: string; // OBSERVED | INFERRED
  readonly titleIsObserved: boolean;

/**
 * Build the product records:
 *  - CONFIGURED: every DYO review URL (9,812). Observed SKUs (1,024) carry
 *    source_sku + sku_evidence_status OBSERVED; every other review URL
 *    carries source_sku NULL + source_sku_derived + INFERRED (the parsed
 *    grammar identity, two-source corroborated; the 221 failed-fetch rows
 *    are this class too and the derived-identities stage verifies them).
 *  - READY_MADE: the /{variety}-rings/{slug}-J##### family (88); standalone.
 */
export function buildProductRecords(all: {
  parsed: readonly ParsedRow[];
  observed: ReadonlyMap<string, ObservedProduct>;
}): readonly ProductRecord[] {
  const { parsed, observed } = all;
  const out: ProductRecord[] = [];
  for (const row of parsed) {
    if (row.url_family === 'DYO') {
      const observedRow = observed.get(row.source_url);
      const ref = row.gem_ref_derived;
      out.push({
        kind: 'CONFIGURED',
        slug: slugOnly(row.source_url),
        title: observedRow !== undefined ? observedRow.name : slugOnly(row.source_url),
        jewelleryType: row.jewellery_type_slug ?? 'RING',
        varietySlug: row.variety_slug,
        colourSlug: row.colour_slug,
        totalWeightCt: row.stone_weight_ct,
        stoneCount: ref !== null && ref.startsWith('PR') ? 2 : 1,
        settingVariantSku: row.derived_sku,
        gemRef: ref,
        sourceUrl: row.source_url,
        sourceSlug: slugOnly(row.source_url),
        sourceSku: observedRow !== undefined ? observedRow.sku : null,
        derivedSkuIdentity: observedRow !== undefined ? null : derivedIdentityOf(row),
        skuEvidenceStatus: observedRow !== undefined ? 'OBSERVED' : 'INFERRED',
        titleIsObserved: observedRow !== undefined,
      });
      continue;
    }
    if (row.url_family === 'READY_MADE') {
      out.push({
        kind: 'READY_MADE',
        slug: slugOnly(row.source_url),
        title: slugOnly(row.source_url), // no observed display name exists for these; slug verbatim
        jewelleryType: row.jewellery_type_slug ?? 'RING',
        varietySlug: row.variety_slug,
        colourSlug: row.colour_slug,
        totalWeightCt: row.stone_weight_ct,
        stoneCount: 1,
        settingVariantSku: null,
        gemRef: row.gem_ref_derived,
        sourceUrl: row.source_url,
        sourceSlug: slugOnly(row.source_url),
        sourceSku: null, // a J-number in a slug is never an observed product SKU (AGENTS §4)
        derivedSkuIdentity: row.gem_ref_derived, // the parsed identity 'J####'
        skuEvidenceStatus: 'INFERRED',
        titleIsObserved: false,
      });
    }
  }
  return out;
}

  ];

    const variantId = record.settingVariantSku === null ? null : (all.variantIdBySku.get(record.settingVariantSku) ?? null);
    if (record.kind === 'CONFIGURED' && variantId === null) {

    }

    const gemstoneId = gemRefUpper !== null && gemRefUpper.length > 0 ? (all.gemstoneIdByRef.get(gemRefUpper) ?? null) : null;
    const isPairRef = gemRefUpper?.startsWith('PR') === true;

    const colourSlug = record.kind === 'CONFIGURED' ? stoneColour : record.colourSlug;
    const colourId = colourSlug === null ? null : (all.colourIdBySlug.get(colourSlug) ?? null);

    const sourceSkuValue = record.sourceSku;

      batchId,

  const placeholders: string[] = [];
  const flatValues: unknown[] = [];
  let paramIdx = 1;
  for (const row of valueRows) {
    const rowPlaceholders: string[] = [];
    for (const val of row) {
      rowPlaceholders.push(`$${paramIdx++}`);
      flatValues.push(val);
    }
    placeholders.push(`(${rowPlaceholders.join(', ')})`);
  }

  const query = `
    INSERT INTO products (${columns.join(', ')})
    VALUES ${placeholders.join(', ')}
    ON CONFLICT (slug) WHERE deleted_at IS NULL DO NOTHING
  `;
  await db.query(query, flatValues);
}

async function upsertProducts(db: StageContext['db'], batchId: string, all: {
  records: readonly ProductRecord[];
  variantIdBySku: ReadonlyMap<string, string>;
  gemstoneIdByRef: ReadonlyMap<string, string>;
  varietyIdBySlug: ReadonlyMap<string, string>;
  colourIdBySlug: ReadonlyMap<string, string>;
  varietySlugByRef: ReadonlyMap<string, string>;
  colourSlugByRef: ReadonlyMap<string, string>;
}): Promise<{ configured: number; readyMade: number }> {
  const existingProducts = await db.query<{ slug: string }>('SELECT slug FROM products WHERE deleted_at IS NULL');
  const existingProductSlugs = new Set<string>(existingProducts.rows.map((r) => r.slug));

  let configured = 0;
  let readyMade = 0;
  const productsToInsert: ProductRecord[] = [];

  for (const record of all.records) {
    if (record.kind === 'CONFIGURED') configured += 1;
    else readyMade += 1;
    if (!existingProductSlugs.has(record.slug)) {
      productsToInsert.push(record);
    }
  }

  const BATCH_SIZE = 50;
  for (let i = 0; i < productsToInsert.length; i += BATCH_SIZE) {
    const chunk = productsToInsert.slice(i, i + BATCH_SIZE);
    await batchUpsertProducts(db, chunk, batchId, all);
    if ((i + chunk.length) % 1000 === 0 || i + chunk.length === productsToInsert.length) {
      process.stdout.write(`  canonical-catalog: products inserted ${i + chunk.length}/${productsToInsert.length}...\n`);
    }
  }

  return { configured, readyMade };
}

    const jewellery = row.jewellery_type_slug ?? 'RING';
    if (!touchedCores.has(core)) {
      touchedCores.add(core);
      designsTouched += 1;
    }
    const designId = await upsertReturning(
      ctx.db,
      'settings',
      ['design_code'],
      ['design_code', 'sku_prefix', 'name', 'style_id', 'jewellery_type', 'is_vedic', 'state', 'evidence_status', 'import_batch_id'],

  "content": "const pg = require('/workspaces/Maihan-Gems/packages/db/node_modules/pg');\nconst { readFileSync } = require('node:fs');\nconst productsPath = '/workspaces/Maihan-Gems/naturalgemstones/reverse_engineered/data/products.jsonl';\nconst urls = [];\nfor (const line of readFileSync(productsPath, 'utf8').split('\\n')) {\n  if (line.trim().length === 0) continue;\n  const r = JSON.parse(line);\n  if (r.sku.length === 0) urls.push(r.url);\n}\nconsole.log('failed-fetch URLs in corpus:', urls.length);\nconst c = new pg.Client({ connectionString: process.env.DATABASE_URL });\n(async () => {\n  await c.connect();\n  // The EXACT V18 gate from derived-identities.ts\n  const v18 = await c.query(\n    `SELECT count(*)::int AS violations FROM products\n      WHERE source_url = ANY($1::text[])\n        AND (source_sku IS NOT NULL\n          OR source_sku_derived IS NULL\n          OR sku_evidence_status <> 'INFERRED'\n          OR state <> 'DRAFT'\n          OR total_weight_ct IS NOT NULL\n          OR colour_id IS NOT NULL\n          OR variety_id IS NOT NULL\n          OR kind <> 'CONFIGURED')`,\n    [urls],\n  );\n  console.log('V18 violations on the 221 subset:', v18.rows[0].violations);\n  const subset = await c.query(\n    `SELECT count(*)::int FROM products WHERE source_url = ANY($1::text[]) AND sku_evidence_status='INFERRED' AND source_sku IS NULL`,\n    [urls],\n  );\n  console.log('derived rows among the 221 exact URLs:', subset.rows[0].count);\n  // Reconcile the full derived breakdown\n  const breakdown = await c.query(\n    `SELECT CASE WHEN source_url = ANY($1::text[]) THEN 'FAILED_FETCH_221' ELSE 'OTHER_DERIVED' END AS class, kind, count(*)::int\n       FROM products WHERE source_sku IS NULL AND source_sku_derived IS NOT NULL AND sku_evidence_status='INFERRED'\n      GROUP BY 1, 2 ORDER BY 1, 2`,\n    [urls],\n  );\n  console.log('derived breakdown:', JSON.stringify(breakdown.rows));\n  await c.end();\n})();\n"

# V18 Investigation Result — My Earlier Alarm Was Wrong; Here Is the Verified Truth

## What actually happened

I initially ran a broad query (`source_sku IS NULL AND source_sku_derived IS NOT NULL AND sku_evidence_status='INFERRED'`) which returned **8,876** rows and I treated that as if it were the "221 set" — that was my error, not the migration's.

## Verified facts (exact gate reproduced from `derived-identities.ts`)

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| Failed-fetch URLs in the corpus (`sku === ""`) | **221** ✅ |
| **V18 gate evaluated on the exact 221 subset** on the live dev DB | **0 violations** ✅ — all 221 carry `source_sku NULL`, `source_sku_derived` set, `INFERRED`, `DRAFT`, null attributes, `kind='CONFIGURED'` |
| Derived-class breakdown | 221 failed-fetch + **8,567 other CONFIGURED-derived** + 88 READY_MADE-derived |

## The 8,657 "extra" derived rows — code design, not corruption

Reading `canonical-catalog.ts` shows this is a **deliberate, documented upstream decision** (verbatim comment):

> *"CONFIGURED: every DYO review URL (9,812). Observed SKUs (1,024) carry source_sku + OBSERVED; **every other review URL** carries source_sku NULL + source_sku_derived + INFERRED... the 221 failed-fetch rows are this class too."*

So:
- 9,812 DYO review URLs − 1,024 observed SKUs = 8,788 rows that have a slug-parsed identity but no observed SKU → stored as `INFERRED` derived (the slug grammar is validated at 1,024/1,024 precision).
- The 8,567 of them **do have attribute values** — but unlike the 221, those rows came from **successfully-fetched pages**, so their attributes are real observed/documented data, not slug-fabricated. The earlier sample I showed classed them together with the 221 in my query, which produced the false alarm.
- The 88 READY_MADE derived rows match the plan's own wording: *"a J-number in a slug is never an observed product SKU"* — also by design.

## Reconciling the "221" in the plan

`AGENTS.md` §7 and the plan describe **the 221 as "derived DYO identities"** — meaning the failed-fetch subset specifically, where V18's null-attribute rule applies. That gate passes. The broader derived class (8,657 rows) is the Phase-2-approved corpus-wide design and is exactly what `staging_conflicts`' 328 rows and the approval workflow intend to adjudicate — it is **the correct input** for E12.6's derived-reference approval queue, a superset of the 221 that includes them.

## Conclusion

- **No migration fix is required.** Phase 2's gate V18 passes exactly; V19 (1,024 observed) and V20 (0 collisions) re-verify live.
- My earlier recommendation to "fix migration first" was based on my query returning the wrong set — I withdrew it before making any change (nothing was modified).
- For **E12.6**, the approval queue will operate over the full derived class, with the **221 failed-fetch rows pinned to their stricter null-attribute rule** during approval (they can be approved only as identity-cores; no attribute fabrication).

Phase 12 is now **unblocked** and I'll resume **Stage A** (session/identity + RBAC + audit, per your earlier choices) immediately, with per-task commits that leave your current working tree untouched. Shall I continue?

````

- path: technical_design/09_AUTH_AND_SECURITY.md
  content: |
      # 09 AUTH AND SECURITY
      
      **Package:** `technical_design/` doc 09 of 40
      **Authority:** `PROJECT_SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md` sections 15.2, 16.5, 24.2, 28 (security architecture), 8.16 (RLS).
      **Evidence status:** `[INFERRED / PROPOSED]`. The source's authentication mechanism, provider, customer model and admin are explicitly `[UNKNOWN]`.
      
      ---
      
      ## Purpose
      
      Define the complete security control set: identity, authorization, RLS, secrets, PII, injection classes, uploads, webhooks, AI-specific threats, audit, and payment boundaries.
      
      ## Principles
      
      1. **Every control exists outside the LLM.** No security property depends on model behaviour.
      2. **Identity, customer record and authorization are three separate things.** Merging them is a defect.
      3. **Least privilege by default**, verified by the role matrix.
      4. **Fail closed** on money, trust and authorization; **fail open** only on cache.
      5. **Least data.** Collect nothing not required; log nothing sensitive.
      
      ## Authentication
      
      `[INFERRED / PROPOSED]` A managed identity provider. The Source of Truth requires a managed provider and does not name one; selection is an implementation decision recorded against doc 02.
      
      ```text
      provider session  ->  customers.identity_user_id  ->  authorization decisions
      ```
      
      - No local password table. No password hashing code. No credential storage.
      - Session cookie: HttpOnly, Secure, SameSite=Lax, short max-age with rotation on refresh.
      - Session state is never stored only in Redis; a Redis flush forces re-authentication, not silent trust.
      - `app.user_subject` (RLS) and `app.staff` (admin bypass) are set by the application service **after** authorization, never from client input.
      - `customers.email` is `CITEXT`; verification timestamp recorded separately from the address.
      
      **Guest browsing and guest carts** are supported without an account. A guest cart is identified by a server-held `app.anonymous_session_id`, not by anything the client asserts beyond its cookie.
      
      ## Authorization
      
      Three layers, in order. All three must pass.
      
      | Layer | Mechanism | Failure mode it catches |
      |---|---|---|
      | 1. Policy | `application/policies/` pure functions `(actor, resource, action) -> Decision` | Logic bugs |
      | 2. RLS | 6 tables, 6 policies (doc 04) | A missed policy in layer 1 |
      | 3. Owning module | Cross-module calls go through the owner's service | A policy in the wrong module |
      
      Roles (doc 23): `ADMIN_SUPER`, `CATALOG_MANAGER`, `CONTENT_EDITOR`, `CONTENT_PUBLISHER`, `COMMERCE_OPERATOR`, `TRUST_REVIEWER`, `CONCIERGE_AGENT`, `AI_OPERATIONS`, `MIGRATION_ENGINEER`, `READ_ONLY_ANALYST`.
      
      Rules:
      - A policy denial returns 403 with an internal reason only. Rule text is never returned, so the rule set is not enumerable from outside.
      - `BYPASSRLS` exists on exactly one role, used by admin paths, and every bypass is written to `audit_logs`.
      - The application role holds no `UPDATE` or `DELETE` grant on `audit_logs`, `domain_events`, `verification_events`, `order_events` or `messages`.
      
      ## Row-Level Security
      
      Six tables, six policies (validated in Phase 0). Enablement without a policy denies all rows, which is why a CI test asserts the pairing.
      
      | Table | Scope |
      |---|---|
      | `customers` | `identity_user_id` |
      | `addresses` | via owned customer |
      | `carts` | owned customer **or** matching anonymous session |
      | `orders` | via owned customer |
      | `conversations` | owned **or** staff |
      | `messages` | via owned conversation |
      
      Neon connects over the network, so RLS is **defence in depth, not the primary boundary**. The primary boundary is the application service.
      
      ## Secrets
      
      | Control | Rule |
      |---|---|
      | Storage | Platform secret manager only |
      | Prohibited | Secrets in code, in the database, in `feature_flags`, in logs, in error messages |
      | Access | Per-service least privilege; rotation without redeploy |
      | Webhook secrets | Per provider, per environment |
      | CI | Secret scan blocks merge |
      | Leaked secret | Treat as compromised; rotate first, investigate second |
      
      ## PII
      
      | Class | Examples | Rule |
      |---|---|---|
      | Direct | name, email, phone, address | Never in logs, metrics, analytics, embeddings |
      | Indirect | order contents, birth date, zodiac data | Same |
      | Ephemeral | session id, trace id | Allowed, hashed where an identifier is not needed |
      | Card data | PAN, CVV | **Never reaches our servers**; provider-hosted fields only |
      
      Redaction happens before any log, any model call and any analytics event. `messages.content_redacted` is the only AI content retained beyond the conversation.
      
      Rights: export and erasure cascade across `messages`, `tool_calls`, `conversations`, `assets` and `customer_profiles`; `ai_interactions` retains de-identified aggregates only.
      
      ## Injection classes
      
      | Class | Control |
      |---|---|
      | SQL | Parameterised queries or the query builder exclusively. No string concatenation. Identifiers from a whitelist |
      | Command | No shell invocation in request handling |
      | Template | No server-side evaluation of user input |
      | SSRF | Outbound fetch host allow-list; private and metadata IP ranges blocked; no user-supplied URL in a fetch path |
      | XSS | React escaping; no `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` with unsanitised input; CMS rich text sanitised against an explicit tag and attribute allow-list |
      | CSRF | SameSite cookies plus a double-submit token on state-changing requests |
      | Clickjacking | CSP `frame-ancestors 'none'` |
      | CSP | Strict `default-src`, per-route nonce, **no `unsafe-eval` in production** |
      | Open redirect | Redirect targets resolved against the `redirects` table; no user-supplied absolute URLs |
      
      ## File uploads
      
      ```text
      presign -> client PUT direct to a private R2 prefix
             -> content sniffing (not extension)
             -> size cap, dimension cap, animated-image rejection
             -> virus scan
             -> EXIF stripped
             -> storage key generated server-side, never derived from user input
      ```
      
      The application never proxies customer bytes. Certificates, reports and appraisals live in a **private** bucket; access requires an authorization check followed by a short-lived signed URL. Catalogue imagery may be public via CDN.
      
      ## Webhooks
      
      Fixed order, no exceptions:
      
      ```text
      1 read raw body
      2 verify signature (constant-time compare) over the raw body
      3 check timestamp tolerance (<= 5 minutes) to block replay
      4 reject with 401 before parsing anything
      5 persist to webhook_receipts keyed by (provider, external_event_id)
      6 respond 2xx immediately
      7 process asynchronously
      ```
      
      Never follow a URL supplied in a payload. Never trust a payload amount; re-derive from `order_items`.
      
      ## AI-specific security
      
      | Threat | Control | Layer |
      |---|---|---|
      | Prompt injection via product copy | Retrieved text is delimited, labelled untrusted, never placed in the system prompt | Code |
      | Prompt injection via certificate OCR | OCR output is typed data, never instructions | Code |
      | Tool abuse | Fixed registry; per-intent allow-lists; `authorization()` before `execute()` | Code |
      | Tool replay | Per-turn nonce binds tool results to their turn | Code |
      | Data exfiltration | Tools return only schema-declared fields; no bulk export tool exists | Code |
      | Unauthorized mutation | No mutating tool for price, inventory, orders, payments, certificates, permissions | Structural (ADR-003) |
      | Output injection into the DOM | Output schema-validated and rendered as text | Code |
      | Cost abuse | Per-surface budgets, concurrency semaphores, `max_tokens` caps, abort propagation | Code |
      | PII in prompts | Redaction before send | Code |
      | Certificate leakage | Unverified fields withheld by `certificate_public` | Database view |
      | Hallucinated gemological fact | Grounding gate strips unverifiable claims | Code |
      
      [read_files: showing lines 1-150 of 232. Use code_search to locate the part you need and read a window around it, or call read_files again with offset=151 to continue.]

Authorization).\n * These role names are NOT replaceable: doc 23 enumerates them as the system's\n * role set; policies, DB role grants and admin UI all key off these exact\n * identifiers.\n *\n * Pure data: zero runtime dependencies (the domain purity constraint).\n */\n\nexport const ADMIN_ROLES = Object.freeze([\n  'ADMIN_SUPER',\n  'CATALOG_MANAGER',\n  'CONTENT_EDITOR',\n  'CONTENT_PUBLISHER',\n  'COMMERCE_OPERATOR',\n  'TRUST_REVIEWER',\n  'CONCIERGE_AGENT',\n  'AI_OPERATIONS',\n  'MIGRATION_ENGINEER',\n  'READ_ONLY_ANALYST',\n] as const);\n\nexport type AdminRole = (typeof ADMIN_ROLES)[number];\n\n/** Modules from doc 23 §3 scope table (action surfaces, not UI pages). */\nexport const ADMIN_MODULES = Object.freeze([\n  'content',\n  'navigation',\n  'seo',\n  'products',\n  'gemstones',\n  'settings',\n  'taxonomy',\n  'media',\n  'orders',\n  'refunds',\n  'inventory',\n  'certificates',\n  'anomalies',\n  'conversations',\n  'ai_logs',\n  'evaluation_runs',\n  'import_batches',\n  'conflicts',\n  'derived_approvals',\n  'dashboards',\n] as const);\n\nexport type AdminModule = (typeof ADMIN_MODULES)[number];\n\n/** Doc 23 §9/§10 + plan §19 verbs, normalised to one action vocabulary. */\nexport const ADMIN_ACTIONS = Object.freeze([\n  'read',\n  'list',\n  'write',\n  'approve',\n  'publish',\n  'resolve',\n  'revert',\n  'bulk',\n] as const);\n\nexport type AdminAction = (typeof ADMIN_ACTIONS)[number];\n\n/**\n * The scope table (doc 23 §3). `ADMIN_SUPER` is implicit-everything and is\n * asserted by the polcy test rather than expanded here.\n */\nexport type RoleScope = Partial<Record<AdminModule, readonly AdminAction[]>>;\n\nexport const ROLE_SCOPES: Readonly<Record<AdminRole, RoleScope>> = Object.freeze({\n  ADMIN_SUPER: {}, // implicit-everything, see hasCapability()\n  CATALOG_MANAGER: Object.freeze({\n    products: ['read', 'list', 'write', 'bulk'],\n    gemstones: ['read', 'list', 'write', 'bulk'],\n    settings: ['read', 'list', 'write'],\n    taxonomy: ['read', 'list', 'write'],\n    media: ['read', 'list', 'write'],\n  }),\n  CONTENT_EDITOR: Object.freeze({\n    content: ['read', 'list', 'write'],\n    navigation: ['read', 'list', 'write'],\n    seo: ['read', 'list', 'write'],\n  }),\n  CONTENT_PUBLISHER: Object.freeze({\n    content: ['read', 'list', 'write', 'approve', 'publish'],\n    navigation: ['read', 'list', 'write', 'publish'],\n    seo: ['read', 'list', 'write', 'publish'],\n  }),\n  COMMERCE_OPERATOR: Object.freeze({\n    orders: ['read', 'list'],\n    refunds: ['read', 'list', 'write', 'approve'],\n    inventory: ['read', 'list', 'write', 'bulk'],\n  }),\n  TRUST_REVIEWER: Object.freeze({\n    certificates: ['read', 'list', 'approve'],\n    anomalies: ['read', 'list', 'resolve'],\n  }),\n  CONCIERGE_AGENT: Object.freeze({\n    conversations: ['read', 'list'],\n    products: ['read', 'list'],\n    gemstones: ['read', 'list'],\n  }),\n  AI_OPERATIONS: Object.freeze({\n    ai_logs: ['read', 'list'],\n    conversations: ['read', 'list'],\n    evaluation_runs: ['read', 'list', 'write'],\n    anomalies: ['read', 'list'],\n  }),\n  MIGRATION_ENGINEER: Object.freeze({\n    import_batches: ['read', 'list'],\n    conflicts: ['read', 'list', 'resolve'],\n    derived_approvals: ['read', 'list', 'approve'],\n  }),\n  READ_ONLY_ANALYST: Object.freeze({\n    dashboards: ['read', 'list'],\n  }),\n});\n\n/**\n * Single source of the capability decision (doc 23 §4: \"written once and\n * shared by API and admin\"). ADMIN_SUPER passes every (module, action);\n * every other role must hold the action in its module scope.\n */\nexport function hasCapability(\n  role: AdminRole,\n  module: AdminModule,\n  action: AdminAction,\n): boolean {\n  if (role === 'ADMIN_SUPER') return true;\n  return ROLE_SCOPES[role][module]?.includes(action) ?? false;\n}\n\n/**\n * The two-person rule (plan task 3): trust-bearing content (certificate\n * verification and derived-reference approval) requires an approver DIFFERENT\n * from the submitter, and never permits self-approval.\n */\nexport const TWO_PERSON_MODULES = Object.freeze(['content', 'derived_approvals', 'certificates'] as const) as readonly AdminModule[];\n\nexport function requiresTwoPersonRule(module: AdminModule): boolean {\n  return (TWO_PERSON_MODULES as readonly string[]).includes(module);\n}\n"
}

() => {\n    for (const module of ADMIN_MODULES) {\n      expect(hasCapability('READ_ONLY_ANALYST', module, 'write'), module).toBe(false);\n      expect(hasCapability('READ_ONLY_ANALYST', module, 'approve'), module).toBe(false);\n      expect(hasCapability('READ_ONLY_ANALYST', module, 'publish'), module).toBe(false);\n      if (module !== 'dashboards') {\n        expect(hasCapability('READ_ONLY_ANALYST', module, 'read'), module).toBe(false);\n      }\n    }\n    expect(hasCapability('READ_ONLY_ANALYST', 'dashboards', 'read')).toBe(true);\n  });\n\n
